Is that alright—does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Outside of these situations at church, do you find that your worries or behaviors interfere with other parts of your life, like at home, work, or with family and friends?\n\nPatient: Well… a little, I suppose. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I have stepped back from some of the choir things and church volunteering. Sometimes I mix up the plans, or who I was supposed to talk to, and then folks seem uncomfortable or they have to fix what I was handling. I feel bad about that, so I try not to take on as much. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do these behaviors ever cause you a lot of distress or make it hard for you to do things you need or want to do outside of church as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s around church things, Doctor. I mean… I still manage at home, but I have had to step back from choir and some volunteering. I get plans mixed up sometimes, or I forget who I was supposed to speak to, and then people look uncomfortable or someone has to redo what I was meant to handle. I feel bad about that, so I just don’t take on as much now. I hope that’s answering what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any unwanted or intrusive thoughts that keep coming up in your mind, even when you're not at church or involved in these activities?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, Doctor… not really. I mean, I worry some, like anybody, but I haven’t noticed thoughts that just push in over and over when I’m at home or away from church. At least, nothing I would know to call that. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you feel worried or unsure, do you find yourself doing something over and over—like checking, counting, or repeating actions—to feel better or to stop the worry?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I do that, Doctor. I might look back once to make sure I didn’t leave something, just normal like, but I don’t count things or keep repeating something to make the worry stop. At least I haven’t noticed myself doing that. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you had to do something over and over, even if you didn’t want to, because it just felt like you had to?\n\nPatient: No, Doctor, I don’t think so. I can’t recall feeling like I had to keep doing something over and over against my will. I mean, I have my little habits like anyone, but nothing that feels forced like that. Is that alright?\n\nDoctor: When you feel unsure or distressed about something, do you ever feel a strong urge to do something specific—like saying a word, counting, or doing a small action—to make the feeling go away?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t believe so, Doctor. When I get upset or unsure, I might just sit quiet a bit or try to calm myself, but I don’t have a certain word I have to say or counting or a little thing I must do to make it stop. I haven’t noticed anything like that. Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: When you feel uncertain or upset about something, even slightly, do you ever feel a quiet urge to do something specific—like touching an object, arranging something just so, or repeating a phrase in your mind—to help yourself feel more at ease?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, Doctor. I mean, I like things tidy enough, but I don’t feel like I have to touch things or line them up just right to calm down. And I don’t repeat phrases in my head, not that I’ve noticed. I hope that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: When you feel uncertain or upset, even slightly, do you ever feel a quiet need to do something small—like tapping something, repeating a word silently, or making sure things are just right—to help yourself feel more settled, even if you don’t fully realize you're doing it?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I do that, Doctor. Maybe I might straighten something without thinking, but not because I feel I have to or to make myself feel better. I haven’t noticed tapping or repeating words like that. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you feel uncertain or upset, even a little, do you ever feel a quiet need to do something small—like tapping something, repeating a word silently, or making sure things are just right—to help yourself feel more settled, even if you don’t fully realize you're doing it?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, Doctor, I know you asked that already. I don’t think I have a special little thing I do… unless I’m missing it somehow. Is that alright?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Or I’ll fold the same stack of napkins again and again, even when people are waiting on me. It’s embarrassing, and I’m not trying to be difficult.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or upset when you find yourself doing these things at church?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, Doctor… I don’t always notice what I’m feeling right then. Sometimes afterward I feel a little embarrassed, like I hope folks don’t think badly of me. But I can’t say for sure if it’s anxiety. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: When you're doing these things at church, like holding the serving spoon or folding napkins, are you having thoughts that you can't control or feeling like you have to do them a certain way?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d call it thoughts I can’t control, Doctor. It’s more like… I just find myself doing it, and then I’m not quite sure why I kept on. I don’t feel like it has to be just so, not that I’ve noticed anyway. Is that answering what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any thoughts coming into your mind that you don’t want there, that you wish would go away but can’t seem to stop?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think so, Doctor… not like a thought barging in that I can’t make leave. I mean, my mind wanders some, but I haven’t really noticed anything like that. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: When you do these things, like holding the serving spoon or folding napkins repeatedly, is it because you feel you must follow some kind of rule or routine, even if you don’t fully understand why?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s a rule, no… not like I’m telling myself I have to do it a certain way. I just sort of end up doing it, and then later I wonder if I looked foolish. But I can’t say there’s a routine I’m following. Is that alright—does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Outside of these situations at church, do you find that your worries or behaviors interfere with other parts of your life, like at home, work, or with family and friends?\n\nPatient: Well… a little, I suppose. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but I have stepped back from some of the choir things and church volunteering. Sometimes I mix up the plans, or who I was supposed to talk to, and then folks seem uncomfortable or they have to fix what I was handling. I feel bad about that, so I try not to take on as much. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do these behaviors ever cause you a lot of distress or make it hard for you to do things you need or want to do outside of church as well?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s around church things, Doctor. I mean… I still manage at home, but I have had to step back from choir and some volunteering. I get plans mixed up sometimes, or I forget who I was supposed to speak to, and then people look uncomfortable or someone has to redo what I was meant to handle. I feel bad about that, so I just don’t take on as much now. I hope that’s answering what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any unwanted or intrusive thoughts that keep coming up in your mind, even when you're not at church or involved in these activities?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, Doctor… not really.
